#2880. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Specifically, Morris highlighted that, according to Apptopia, Muse reached 1.3 million daily active users in five weeks versus 300,000 in the first week. Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

Next is semiconductor company Marvell Technology. Earlier this month, Marvell held its Investor Day, where it raised its total addressable market estimate to $400 billion by 2030, reflecting solid AI-led demand tailwinds.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

Svanberg ranks No. 91 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 31.8%. See Marvell Technology Statistics on TipRanks.

#2878. It’s time for Basketball: San Antonio Spurs vs Golden State Warriors

Welcome to the Game Thread. Veterans of the Game Thread know how we do things around here, but for all you newbies we have a few rules. Our community guidelines apply and basically say be cool, no personal attacks, don't troll and don't swear too much.

The Spurs are on a double digit win streak and they're back at home tonight after a successful four game road trip. Tonight is an NBA Cup game against the Golden State Warriors for the lead in West Group C. All of the teams in Group C except for the Spurs and Warriors have a loss in the group stage of the Emirates Cup tournament, and one of those teams is going to have a loss after tonight's game. The winner of tonight's game will have the only undefeated record in the group so far, and will win the group outright by winning the last two games. The Spurs' remaining group stage games are at Portland and against the Lakers at home, neither of which counts as an easy win, but a win tonight gives them an inside track on advancing to the knockout rounds and making a little extra money for the guys. Actually, it's a nice payday for the Cup winners, $530,000 for players who are Cup champions, which could be really helpful for the guys on rookie contracts.

The Warriors are missing Steph Curry who tore an oblique on Wednesday night against the Rockets, which means that the best player on the Warriors is going to be super rookie Yaxel Lendeborg, who has been tearing up the league so far this year. Athough Yax is a first year player, he's actually older than three of the starting five players on the Spurs. The Warriors were really smart to draft a player who was ready to contribute immediately to give Draymond and Steph another chance to win a title, and Lendeborg has definitely made an immediate contribution. This is not a game that the Spurs can take for granted, and if they come out with a lackadaisical effort like the first half of the Bulls game, this could be a rough night for the home team. This is a get-back game [first home game after an extended road trip] for the Silver and Black, and those are always dangerous for the home team. Mitch has to have his team ready for tonight, and maybe he should give the halftime speech from Wednesday in the pre-game.

De'Aaron Fox and Devin Vassell are both questionable for tonight's game, so you could see some interesting lineups this evening. This game is on CW 35, DAZN, and Prime, so you have plenty of options to watch. Lets go Spurs, and Wembanyama must dominate Yaxel.

#2871. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

#2866. At least 50 different quarterbacks will have started games through Week 10

Raiders quarterback Fernando Mendoza will make his first career start on Sunday. Two others will make their debuts, too: Cardinals quarterback Carson Beck and Dolphins quarterback Kyle McCord.

Via NBC Sports research, they'll become the 48th, 49th, and 50th different quarterbacks to start games during the 2026 season.

It will be only the second time in the last 15 years (excluding the 1987 strike season) during which 50 different quarterbacks have started games in the first 10 weeks of a season.

If, for some reason, another quarterback makes his first start of the season on Sunday (that's unlikely with Seahawks quarterback Drew Lock not having an injury designation for Sunday), it will be the second-most starters through 10 weeks since 1950.

In 1999, 55 different quarterbacks started games through the first 10 weeks of the season.

Most of the changes have arisen from injury. For the Raiders, the transition to Mendoza happened after Kirk Cousins was traded to the Vikings.

#2862. Federal Reserve issues FOMC statement

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 2:00 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

#2860. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2858. Aaron Glenn has plenty to say about coaching buddies on Bills staff

Aaron Glenn has plenty of familiarity with the Bills coaching staff.

He and Bills coach Joe Brady spent two years together on the same Saints staff under Sean Payton. Glenn was the defensive backs coach, and Brady was an offensive assistant at the time.

"Obviously he's done a really good job, and I'm just talking about this year as the head coach of the team and what they're doing," Glenn said Friday. "We started in New Orleans together with Dan Campbell [who was the assistant head coach and tight ends coach at the time]. We had a really good staff there. Then [Brady] actually ended up going to LSU and being a part of that staff when everything blew up with those receivers [Ja'Marr Chase and Justin Jefferson] and quarterbacks [Joe Burrow] they had there. We always stayed in touch, and we talked ball a lot.

"I would take a drive up to Baton Rouge to speak with him about some things that they were doing there, and it was vice versa. He would come back up to New Orleans where we were, and we would talk ball. He's a damn good coach. Really bright offensive mind, and he's doing a really good job with those guys."

Buffalo Bills head coach Joe Brady looks on against the Minnesota Vikings during a game at U.S. Bank Stadium in Minneapolis, Monday, Nov. 9, 2026. Getty Images

Glenn, when he began his NFL coaching career as an assistant defensive backs coach with the Browns, also coached Bills defensive coordinator Jim Leonhard, who had a 10-year NFL career as a defensive back, for one season.

"Very, very smart and intelligent player," Glenn said. "I would say this, and I don't think he gets a lot of credit for this, he was way more athletic than people gave him credit for. I can recall one meeting that we had as coach Penn [Mike Pettine] as our head coach, he showed a clip, a couple of clips of Jim actually dunking a basketball. I think the guys got a kick out of that. He was 5-foot-7 or something like that, 5-foot-8 dunking a basketball the way he was doing it. The guys got a kick out of doing it."

Jets head coach Aaron Glenn reacts on the sideline during a game against the Las Vegas Raiders in East Rutherford, N.J., Sunday, Nov. 1, 2026. Bill Kostroun for NY nPost

Harrison Phillips was in the same 2018 Bills draft class as Josh Allen. He had a front-row seat for Allen's development into a superstar.

"I think I really did see a lot of those same traits back then that he still has now," Phillips said Friday. "He was elite when I first saw him. I wouldn't even say confidence — I think at the time, he knew how good of a player he was. We didn't win a lot of games that first year, but he knew what was going on. I don't know, he seems maybe a little bit more fulfilled. I feel like he's smiling a lot more. He's a husband now, he's a father now. Maybe just feeling a little bit more complete."

Linebacker Trevin Wallace (groin) is the only Jets player ruled out for Sunday's game. Edge rusher Joseph Ossai, who has missed the last four games with an ankle injury, is listed as questionable but was a full participant in Friday's practice.

The Bills are getting back tackle Dion Dawkins and guard Alec Anderson, who both missed their win over the Vikings last week, on Sunday. But they'll likely be without tackle Spencer Brown (ankle), who is doubtful.

#2851. Malik Nabers’ tough week has him stewing on costly mistakes that ‘could’ve been the difference’

One of the toughest weeks of Malik Nabers' standout young NFL career was compounded by a "family emergency back home."

With the Giants in the facility Friday for meetings but not practice, Nabers said that he wasn't in the locker room after Thursday's game for personal reasons — not because of his frustrations after losing a fumble for the first time in three years and not preventing an interception on the final pass of a 24-21 loss to the Commanders.

"You are thinking about plays that could've been the difference in the game," Nabers said. "That [fumble] could've been one of those. We could've got a field goal or a touchdown. Sadly, went away just off a mistake I made. Just try to be better, and I'll be better on protecting the football and having awareness of where I am."

Nabers is close friends with former LSU teammate Jayden Daniels, who improved to 4-0-1 against the Giants as Commanders quarterback.

"It's bugging me pretty bad," Nabers said. "He calls me all the time. He's probably trying to call me right now. But I can't answer that phone call."

Washington Commanders cornerback Rasul Douglas (29) intercepts a pass intended for New York Giants wide receiver Malik Nabers (1) on Nov. 13, 2026. Charles Wenzelberg / New York Post

Nabers laid flat on his back on the turf at the end of the game after Rasul Douglas intercepted a jump ball. He always encourages his quarterbacks to just give him a chance, but J.J. McCarthy could've thrown elsewhere against a zero blitz when the Giants needed to gain only 20 yards over the next 34 seconds to attempt a 50-yard field goal.

"I saw the ball go up," Nabers said. "I didn't really see [Douglas] or where he was so I'm not really aware. My head is looking up at the ball, even though he was driving on the ball. Just seeing how high the ball was and waiting for it to come down, made a jump to catch the ball and got hit, and he made a play."

New York Giants wide receiver Malik Nabers (1) catches a pass against Washington Commanders cornerback Amik Robertson (2) on Nov. 12, 2026, in East Rutherford. Robert Sabo for NY Post

Nabers was criticized last week when his body language against the Eagles was critiqued by the Fox broadcast team of Joe Davis and Greg Olsen. He fired back twice after interpreting their words as suggesting he is a bad teammate.

There was a time Thursday when Nabbers hopped up and down and gestured to the sideline after an incompletion to Isaiah Likely. He had two catches for 3 yards at the time.

But Nabers also hustled to block for Likely on his 66-yard touchdown catch. The two danced together in the end zone. But the celebration soon turned into disappointment. How do you move forward?

"I'm still trying to find ways," Nabers said. "I take losses more painful than a lot of people. I want to win so bad. When I receive a loss, it hurts. … You just have to be able to flush it, move on and do everything you can the next week to try to win. It's hard, for sure. I don't know how to handle that. I'm still going through that. I'm still learning to find my way on flushing losses like this — or losses in general."

#2845. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.â¯â¯

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

20th Street and Constitution Avenue N.W., Washington, DC 20551

#2844. Game Day: Louisville at North Carolina

Location: Kenan Stadium: Chapel Hill, N.C.

Announcers: Mike Couzens (play-by-play), Tom Luginbill (analyst), and Lauren Sisler (sidelines)

Last Meeting: Louisville won 47-35 on Sept. 9, 2017 in Chapel Hill

Bill Belichick had a poor start to his tenure at UNC last year, with the main issues being on the offensive side of the ball. He remedied that issue this year by going out and hiring none other than Bobby Petrino to be his offensive coordinator. Petrino had been bouncing around at a few spots since being fired by Louisville, but his offensive acumen is still coveted. The Tar Heels have improved on offense, though they still rank toward the bottom of the rankings in some key offensive metrics.

As always, an offense is only as good as its quarterback, and the Tar Heels are average with Billy Edwards under center. The veteran transfer quarterback has played about as well as most expected him to this season. His inconsistent play has been an issue for this offense, and it has contributed to a couple of the losses this year.

As the season has gone on, he has played better, with his last two games being his best. He torched Miami's defense for four touchdowns while completing 82% of his passes. He came back the following weekend to complete 76% of his passes against a solid UConn team. Edwards isn't one of the top quarterbacks Louisville has seen this season, but he has played like one recently.

UNC has three running backs that they can rely on, and they play all three of them. Demon June leads the group in touchdowns with 8 on the season. Benjamin Hall leads the group in yards and yards per carry. Jaylin Mcgill leads the group in yards per play as he is a factor as a receiver out of the backfield. They are all over 220 pounds, and Petrino has done a good job of utilizing them this season. The staff likes to ride the hot hand with this group, so we will have to wait to see which player gets the most touches.

Louisville will face another opponent that has a single key player at the wide receiver position this weekend. Jordan Shipp is one of the more talented players the Cards will face this year, and they will need to have a plan. Shipp has good size at 6-2/200, and he can really move. He is a fluid athlete with good body control which he uses to make contested catches and catches in tight spots. He is not a burner in the open field, but he has plenty of speed to score on a long play if he gets open.

The biggest threat on this offense is at tight end in Jelani Thurman. Lincoln Keinholz' former teammate at Ohio State has had a similar situation as he did not make much of an impact for the Buckeyes, but he has emerged as one of the best tight ends in the country for UNC. He has already set the school record for touchdowns by a tight end with 8, which is the most in the country among tight ends. He also ranks in the top five in just about every receiving category when it comes to tight ends.

This team doesn't create a lot of big passing plays, but when they do it is down the middle of the field to Thurman, or because he pulls defenders with him. Petrino is known for working deep over routes throughout his career, but this team has killed teams down the seams. Louisville's defense has been victimized many times on seam routes this year, so they will need to adjust their plan from their normal reliance on Cover 3.

The UNC offensive line has been pretty average this year, especially from a run blocking standpoint. They don't allow a lot of penetration, but they haven't been opening massive holes for the running backs, either. Where they have struggled is being consistent in pass protection. Billy Edwards is just a big better than being a statue in the pocket, so he is not one to avoid the rush if it gets to him. It has not helped that his line has struggled with speed rushers this year. The Cards should have an opportunity to get pressure this weekend.

Defense is Bill Belichick's forte, and the North Carolina Tar Heels are benefiting from his expertise this year. For the second straight year, Belichick has his defense ranked in the top 50 in yards per play. His group is being led by his son Brian after his son Steve left the team earlier this season. While the defense has taken a step back this year, they have played well enough to keep the Tar Heels in each game, and they've made big plays at big moments.

The most talented group on this team is the defensive line, with a couple of NFL prospects. Melkart Abou Jaoude is the returning ACC leader in sacks, while Jaylen Harvey has been a very good addition from Penn State with 21 pressures this season. The edges of this defense haven't put up crazy numbers from a havoc standpoint, but they will be key players for this defense if Louisville continues to attack the edges of the defense.

The interior of the line is where this defense is at its best. Xavier Lewis is one of the best defensive tackles in the country. He is in the top five in pressures, tackles for loss, and sacks among his position group. The 6-0/260 tackle is extremely undersized, but he is very quick, and he uses his quickness to beat blocks to get into the backfield. He will be a key player in this game and will likely cause some issues for Louisville's offensive line.

Isaiah Johnson and Leroy Jackson are the other top players on the interior of the defense. Jackson has 14 pressures on the season as the main backup for Lewis, but he and Johnson provide really good size on the inside as well. Johnson is big enough to demand a double team, which has been a factor in Lewis' big season. He has been able to get one-on-one matchups often, and that has not gone well for offensive linemen.

UNC's linebackers have benefited more than just about anyone from the strong play of the defensive line. Derek McDonald and Peyton Sheelmann are averaging 19 tackles a game between the two of them. Both players are ranked in the top 25 in tackles nationally, and they are constantly running free when you watch their games. Louisville has adapted their running game over the last few weeks, but they will likely have a hard time running the stretch zone runs that they really like. These two guys can run, and they will have a lot of space to get downhill to make tackles behind the line.

Jaiden Patterson leads a solid cornerback group that has played at an average level this season. UNC has given up a lot of yardage this season, but they are better when it comes to metrics like passer rating. The corners have had some issues against the top players they've seen this year. Umari Hatcher, Jordon Faison, Calvin Russell, and Bryant Wesco all stood out to me when I watched their games. Those receivers were able to get open and make big plays on the outside.

The biggest playmaker outside of the defensive linemen is Greg Smith at free safety. He leads the team with 3 interceptions this year, and he's at his best as a deep safety playing center field. Smith can also come down into the box in run support or cover if needed. He's having one of the best seasons in the ACC at safety. While this secondary isn't great, they do have a couple of players who can make a play on the ball, which is not something I would say about the last few opponents for the Cards.

Not easy to find non-basketball tweets from the UNC crowd.

Give me an SMU win over Wake at noon and a Cards win in Chapel Hill, and the EL is probably back up to where we want it to be for next week's home/conference finale against Pitt.

Game Attire: Red Script Louisville hoodie

Soup season is still going very strong at CC Headquarters.

Bold Prediction: Neither team leads by double figures at any point in this game

Jeff Brohm Trick Play Alert Level: Fresh Air (Moderately above average)

I don't believe for a second that Jeff and Brian don't want to show Bobby something.

Predicted Star of the Game: Lincoln Kienholz

Carolina will sell out to stop the run, and they'll do this despite having a suspect secondary. This feels like it needs to be a Linc game if it's going to be a Cards win.

—Despite Louisville being a member of the ACC since 2014, this will be just the second time U of L has faced North Carolina as a member of the conference. The Cardinals have not faced any other conference opponent fewer times than it's faced the Tar Heels, and league newcomer Cal is the only other ACC opponent Louisville has played just once.

—North Carolina will celebrate both Homecoming and Military Appreciation Night on Saturday.

—NorthCarolina is in its 136th season of football (139th year) and the Tar Heels own an all-time record of 760-588-54.

—Louisville's 44 points at Georgia Tech last week were its most on the road under Jeff Brohm and the most in any road game since a 62-22 throttling at Duke in 2021.

—Louisville's offense enters the weekend ranked sixth nationally in points per game at 40.8.

—Louisville has scored at least 27 points in all nine of its games so far this season. Boise State is the only other FBS team to have accomplished that feat.

—North Carolina offensive coordinator Bobby Petrino did three stints on the Louisville coaching staff, including two as head coach. Petrino coached the Cards from 2003-06 and 2014-18, going 77-35 in nine seasons. Petrino is also the brother of U of L tight ends coach Paul Petrino.

—As the head coach at Louisville, Bobby Petrino was 3-0 in games against North Carolina.

—North Carolina's defense is allowing opponents to convert on 44.1 percent of their third down tries, good for 121st in the country.

—Louisville's pass defense is allowing 265.9 passing yards per game, good for 130th in the country.

—Louisville has won 11 conference road games under Jeff Brohm, tied for the most in the ACC during that time.

—UNC quarterbacks coach Matt Lombardi began his coaching career at Louisville working as a special teams quality control coach in 2014 before working with tight ends as a graduate assistant in 2015-16.

—North Carolina wide receivers coach Garrick McGee has twice previously worked at Louisville. McGee was the offensive coordinator and quarterbacks coach in 2014-15 and then the wide receivers coach under Jeff Brohm from 2023-24.

—Louisville wide receivers coach Deion Branch played seven of his 11 NFL seasons with the New England Patriots for current UNC head coach Bill Belichick. Branch was part of two Super Bowl winning teams in New England, including earning Super Bowl XXXIX MVP honors.

#2842. Devin Booker’s clutch scoring carries Suns amid starting lineup woes

As precious seconds ticked off the clock late in Thursday night's matchup between the Phoenix Suns and Charlotte Hornets, it was abundantly clear that Charlotte was attempting to intentionally foul Devin Booker. And Booker? He was ready and willing to accept that foul.

Unfortunately, head official Scott Foster apparently had his attention elsewhere, despite the action unfolding approximately five feet in front of him. Perhaps he was looking into the front row, spotted Steve Nash, and began reminiscing about the good old days when he and his fellow officials allowed opponents to assault the two-time MVP with little consequence. Maybe he noticed Brandon Rafi sitting courtside and started thinking about calling Rafi Rafi the next time he needs a lawyer. Whatever was occupying his attention, it certainly wasn't the intentional foul taking place directly in front of him.

Booker responded with that familiar piercing stare, the one we've seen countless times when he's wondering how an official could possibly miss something so obvious. But there was still a basketball game to finish, and with Sion James approaching to provide a double team, Booker decided to take matters into his own hands. He dribbled left, rose up, and buried a 28-footer that effectively sealed the victory for Phoenix.

No whistle? No problem. Sometimes the best response to questionable officiating is to make the officials irrelevant. And in that moment, Devin Booker did exactly that.

That moment encapsulated just how great Devin Booker has been to begin this season, with the exception of his three-point performance against Oklahoma City. For a team navigating a discombobulated, duplicative roster that is ultimately going to go as far as Booker can carry it, the Suns' franchise cornerstone has been delivering. Nowhere has that been more evident than in the clutch.

Booker scored nine points in clutch time against Charlotte, continuing an impressive stretch of late-game execution. Through four clutch appearances this season, he's shooting 66.7% from the field, 50% from beyond the arc, and has a +8 plus/minus. Those are encouraging numbers for a player whose late-game efficiency became a legitimate concern last season.

What impressed me most against the Hornets wasn't necessarily the scoring. It was his decision-making. Every time Charlotte attempted to throw a double team his way, Booker recognized what was happening and responded decisively and appropriately. On one possession, he drove away from the pressure, penetrated the defense, and kicked the ball to Jordan Goodwin in the left corner for an open three-point opportunity. Goodwin missed the shot, but the process was exactly what you wanted to see. On another possession, Booker beat the double team himself and converted. He wasn't forcing the issue. He was reading the defense, understanding what was available, and making the appropriate decision.

We are currently witnessing what can only be described as a Devin Booker heater. Over his last 7 games, he's averaging 27.4 points on 49/40/95 splits.

Quite honestly, the Suns desperately need it because the rest of the starting lineup is struggling mightily. Perhaps it's the lack of continuity and limited playing time together. Maybe it's the absence of clearly defined roles, with players still attempting to understand where they fit within an offense that features overlapping skill sets. Whatever the explanation, the production from Phoenix's starters has been inconsistent and inefficient.

Booker has accounted for 35.1% of all points scored by the Suns' starting lineup this season. Think about that. More than one out of every three points produced by the starters has come from a single player. Meanwhile, the starters not named Devin Booker have combined to shoot 52-of-154 from beyond the arc, a pedestrian 33.8%. In the clutch, everyone without 'Booker' written on their back has combined to make 20% of their threes and 30% of their shots.

While the shooting inefficiencies that plagued Booker last season generated plenty of concern, those issues haven't carried over into the early portion of this campaign. Against Charlotte, he connected on 4-of-8 attempts from deep, continuing to provide the offensive production and efficiency that Phoenix needs from its best player. He's up to 25.5 points this season, doing so on 50/38/94 splits, adding 5.0 assists, and 1.2 steals.

Until the rest of the starting five figures out who they are, what their responsibilities should be, and how they can contribute more effectively on both ends of the floor, the Suns are going to need Booker to keep this heater going. Because if he has an ordinary night, there's a pretty good chance Phoenix is going to lose. That's an unfortunate reality, but it's where this organization finds itself.

Devin Booker is in the prime of his career. This should be the period when the franchise has a clear understanding of what works alongside him, what complements his strengths, and how to construct a roster that maximizes his abilities. The organization has had plenty of time to figure that out.

Instead, we've watched Phoenix spend much of his prime fumbling through roster construction, repeatedly acquiring players with overlapping skill sets and similar positional responsibilities. Rather than consistently surrounding Booker with complementary pieces, the organization has continued placing additional weight on his shoulders, asking him to compensate for deficiencies that should have been addressed through better roster construction. Thankfully, at least through the opening weeks of this season, Booker appears more than willing to accept that responsibility.

But how long can he sustain it? How long can a team reasonably expect one player to account for more than a third of its starting lineup's scoring while simultaneously serving as its primary offensive organizer and late-game closer? At some point, the supporting cast has to establish its identity and begin providing consistent production.

I've been quick to remind people that Devin Booker is the $57 million man whenever his performances fall short of expectations. When you're earning that kind of money, there's a certain level of production, leadership, and accountability that comes with the paycheck. And right now? He's earning every damn dollar.

You earn that money by delivering in the clutch, which Booker has done. You earn it by becoming the stabilizing force that prevents an inconsistent team from completely unraveling. Check that too, as the team sits at 5-6. You earn it by showing up night after night, accepting the responsibility of carrying an offense while your teammates are still trying to figure out their roles, their responsibilities, and perhaps why they're being asked to occupy spaces already filled by somebody else. That's what Devin Booker has been doing.

And while I certainly hope the rest of the roster begins providing him with the support he deserves, there's something worth appreciating about watching a player embrace that burden and continue delivering. Because for all the questions surrounding the Phoenix Suns right now, Devin Booker is providing the most important answer. He's playing like the $57 million man.

We've spent plenty of time questioning Booker's late-game execution, his decision-making, and whether he can consistently elevate a flawed roster. Right now, he's answering those questions with his play. The Suns still have plenty to figure out, and Booker can't be expected to carry them indefinitely. But for the moment, he's reminding everyone why he's the face of this franchise. And why, when the game is on the line, you probably shouldn't leave him open from 28 feet.

#2841. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations (PDF)

Agencies seek comment on proposed third-party risk management guidance and issue statement on community bank engagement with core service providers

Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System

#2838. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2835. Jensen Huang scandal casts doubt on Nvidia’s AI dominance — how we got here

Nvidia is facing one the biggest crises in its 33 year history, with founder Jensen Huang resigning as CEO Thursday after multiple reported affairs, including one with a woman alleged to be a Chinese spy. Now CFO Colette Kress has taken the helm as Nvidia's interim CEO.

The implications of Huang's affair with Alice Huang run far deeper than infidelity. That's because her ties to Nvidia customer Megaspeed put her in the middle of a sprawling effort to accumulate AI chips across Southeast Asia. And Megaspeed is under investigation for possibly diverting restricted Nvidia chips into the hands of Chinese companies.

The relationship between Jensen Huang and Alice Huang dates back to 2023, according to The New York Times. That was a "turbulent" period for Nvidia, Jensen Huang told CNBC during an interview at the time. Nvidia was in the midst of redesigning some chips to comply with the first in a series of strict export controls imposed by the U.S. government that restricted sales to China.

"The regulation is going to be just fine. We're going to be able to work through it," Huang told CNBC from Nvidia's headquarters in February 2023. The interview was for a documentary about how Huang created Nvidia and built it into the backbone of the AI boom.

That same day, CNBC also interviewed Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of healthcare, with whom Jensen Huang reportedly had an affair.

It's not the first time Nvidia has faced an uncertain future. The company nearly went bankrupt in the early years after it was founded in 1993.

"Every company makes mistakes and I make a lot of them," Huang said in the 2023 interview. "And some of them put the company in peril."

In the early months of the AI boom, after OpenAI launched ChatGPT in November 2022, Nvidia was on the precipice of historic growth. Jensen Huang told CNBC he'd like to keep running the company for "three or four decades," as long as "I feel that I'm making a real contribution to the company."

Less than four years later, Jensen Huang is out, and we're taking a look back at our interviews with the Nvidia co-founder and other company executives as the chipmaker enters this next critical chapter. Watch the video for the full story.

#2828. Trump says he wants to ‘get Cuba done’ before leaving office, would consider military force

U.S. President Donald Trump has said he wants to "get Cuba done" before leaving office in 2029, adding that he would "certainly consider" military intervention against the communist-run Caribbean island.

In an interview with conservative radio host Hugh Hewitt on Thursday, Trump said his administration was monitoring the situation in Havana "very closely" and described Cuba as a "failed" state.

"I want to get Cuba done before I leave," Trump said. His comments marked the first time he has put a timeline on possible military intervention to topple Cuban authorities, according to Politico.

Trump said Secretary of State Marco Rubio, whose parents are from Cuba, "would love to do it" and said he would "certainly consider" a short military operation.

It comes as the U.S. continues to enforce a comprehensive economic embargo against Cuba, a campaign which has contributed to a deepening energy and economic crisis on the island. Cuba has suffered repeated nationwide blackouts in recent months, as well as fuel and food shortages.

The Cuban Embassy in Washington, D.C., estimated on Thursday that the cost of the U.S. blockade to Cuba between March 2025 and February 2026 amounted to $8 billion.

Earlier this month, the Treasury Department imposed further sanctions on Cuban oil tankers and shipping companies and threatened to target any international entity or person doing business within its marine or maritime industries as part of a push "to end the Cuban regime's malign activities, both in Cuba and across our hemisphere."

In response, Cuba's Embassy in Washington, D.C., said these steps "deepen an energy siege that already leaves hospitals without reliable power and families facing blackouts. The blockade is not targeted at a government. It is collective punishment that hits ordinary people every day."

Trump has previously talked up the prospect of a "friendly takeover" of Havana and said the White House could turn its sights on Cuba after the Iran war. The U.S. president has also said he could do anything he wanted with the country, adding that he thinks he will have the "honor" of "taking Cuba."

In May, Cuban Energy Minister Vicente de la O Levy said the country of roughly 10 million had run out of fuel oil and diesel, describing the country's predicament as "extremely tense."

Analysts have typically been skeptical about the prospect of a U.S. military campaign against Cuba, suggesting that further economic pressure against the island's authorities appears more likely.

#2827. Rapid Recap: Magic 107, Bucks 99

The Milwaukee Bucks fall to 0/2 in the NBA Cup and 1-12 overall, losing to the Orlando Magic 107-99 in a game that was at least competitive. Jaime Jaquez Jr. led the Bucks yet again with 22 points off the bench on 8/15 shooting and 5/7 from three. Franz Wagner starred for the Magic, dropping a near triple-double of 35 points, 12 assists, and nine rebounds (at one point it was a triple-double, but they apparently retracted a rebound).

With Ousmane Dieng getting the surprise start, the Bucks began slowly, with Myles Turner turning the ball over twice in the first two possessions and the team going 1/5 from the field and 1/4 from the charity stripe as the Magic led 12-4 at the seven-minute mark. Milwaukee responded, however, with a 10-3 run, thanks in large part to Kel'el Ware, who impacted early with a put-back slam and a corner triple. We even got treated to first-quarter Gary Trent Jr. minutes, but his elite defence couldn't stop Franz Wagner, who carved up the visitors in the pick-and-roll as the Magic took a 23-15 lead into the first major break.

Wagner continued to kill the Bucks early in the second, nailing two threes to extend Orlando's lead to 13 just over two minutes in as Milwaukee's shooting woes continued. Thankfully, a select few Bucks were shooting well, including Myles Turner, who nailed his third three of the night to cut the deficit to 10 with seven minutes to play. After starting 0/5, Tyler Herro began to cook as the second wore on, hitting a three, a floater, and a few free throws as the Bucks inched closer, down 34-28 halfway through the period. Unfortunately, the Magic closed on an 18-8 run as their balanced attack dominated the Bucks on both ends, leading 52-36 at the half.

Coach Jenkins started Nate Ament in Dieng's place to start the third, and the rookie paid that faith off, opening the quarter with a corner three after going 1/6 in the first half. In general, the Bucks looked much more fluid on offence, but they couldn't get stops as Desmond Bane punished them twice from deep to give his side a 65-45 edge four minutes in. The visitors wouldn't give in, though; they knuckled down on defence, which allowed them to play more in transition and go on a 10-0 run. That said, the Bucks had major free throw issues, making just 7/15 (!) midway through the third. That's when Bane really started to cook, knocking in a three plus the foul, then another three on the next possession as the margin blew back out to 16. Jaime Jaquez Jr. was huge to close the quarter, though, nailing multiple bombs and a mid-range shot to keep the Bucks within shouting distance, down 84-72 after three.

It was a frantic start to the fourth, with Milwaukee giving up plenty of open looks but the Magic failing to hit any; as a result, the visitors kept their deficit around 10 as they lived at the line. The Magic's lead stayed around that margin for the next four minutes as the Bucks' parade to the charity stripe continued (though they kept missing when they got there). In fact, Milwaukee didn't hit a field goal for over six minutes, but remarkably, they remained (somewhat) in the game. In the end, though, Wagner was too much, leading his side to their first home victory of the season.

The Bucks were atrocious from the free throw line, making just 18 of 31 attempts in a game they lost by eight. Kyle Kuzma shot a staggering 7/15 on his own!

I physically cannot watch Kuzma-ball anymore, man. It's buyout time (even though I get that he has no incentive to want that).

#2826. An Eagles rookie progress report at the midway point of 2026 season

An Eagles rookie progress report at the midway point of 2026 season originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Eagles had high expectations entering this season but are sitting at 2-7 at the bye week.

Now it's time to see what the young players can do.

The Eagles drafted eight players in April and seven of them have seen game action over the first half of the season, but to varying degrees.

With the Eagles on their Week 10 bye week, let's recap the first half of the season for all eight of their draft picks:

After some disappointing moments early in his rookie season, we have seen a ton of growth from Makai Lemon recently. Without DeVonta Smith and Dontayvion Wicks for three weeks, Lemon has had the three best games of his rookie season against the Cowboys (7 catches for 67 yards, 1 TD), Commanders (6 catches for 57 yards) and Giants (6 catches for 61 yards). While Lemon was tough on himself for a couple drops in Week 9, he has been one of the few bright spots recently.

Through nine games, Lemon has been targeted 54 times and has 37 catches for 312 yards and a touchdown. He's on pace for 102 targets, 70 catches, 589 yards and 2 touchdowns. But it's also worth noting that his numbers have been improving as the season goes on.

Here's a look at the Eagles' rookie records for receptions and receiving yards. Lemon is on pace to finish his season second on the receptions list and ninth in receiving yards. But hopefully the Eagles have scrapped all those behind-the-line passes to him.

Through nine weeks, Lemon is tied with KC Conception for the lead among rookies in receptions and is fourth in receiving yards among rookies behind Denzel Boston (507), Concepcion (437) and Carnell Tate (413).

After a rough training camp, Eli Stowers dealt with a quad injury that kept him on IR for the first six weeks of the season. He made his NFL debut a few weeks ago against the Cowboys, but hasn't played a ton on offense yet. Here's a look at his offensive snap counts for these last three weeks.

Week 7 vs. DAL: 12 snapsWeek 8 at WAS: 13 snapsWeek 9 vs. NYG: 7 snaps

Overall, Stowers has been targeted 5 times and has 3 catches for 53 yards. All three of those catches came in the loss to Washington and two of them came very late in the fourth quarter. His long of 34 yards came at the very end of the game.

But Stowers hasn't looked out of place in limited action. He has been doing some good things away from the football and has shown a real willingness to do some dirty work and block. While blocking is probably never going to be his strength, he needed to improve in that area and is clearly working on it. But in this lost season, it would make sense to see more of Stowers in the second half.

In addition to his work on offense, Stowers has also played 27 special teams snaps. That's something new for him because he began his college career as a quarterback and then became a star tight end. But he's working hard at that too.

The plan was for Markel Bell to sit all season and learn behind Jordan Mailata and Lane Johnson. But that plan got tossed in the garbage can early this season when Johnson abruptly retired. Bell was thrust into the starting lineup in Week 4 against the Rams and has started the last six games with plenty of ups and downs.

Early in his time as a starter, Bell looked really promising and looked to be settling into that role. But the last couple of games have been much rougher for the 6-foot-9 rookie. In six starts this season, according to ProFootballFocus, Bell has given up 5 sacks and 19 pressures, but 3 of those sacks came in his late-notice start against the Rams in Week 4.

There's a really good chance that veteran Fred Johnson would be a better option right now, but the Eagles should ride this out with Bell to figure out if he really has the potential to be a long-term replacement for Johnson. We'll probably learn a lot about him over the second half of this season.

Round 5-178: Cole Payton, QB, North Dakota State

The Eagles made sure to give Cole Payton plenty of reps in training camp despite having three quarterbacks on the depth chart ahead of him. But Payton has been inactive five times this season and has been active but hasn't played three times. Back in Week 5 against the Jaguars, Payton played 10 snaps on special teams and that's the only time he has seen the field.

The idea of Payton on special teams is fun, especially in a lost season like this. Maybe the Eagles will get him some more reps in that role or perhaps find a way to get him on the field on offense. Joe Milton and Tanner McKee are both still ahead of Payton on the depth chart but the way this season is going, would you be surprised to see Payton eventually get on the field as a starter?

When the Eagles drafted Micah Morris in the sixth round, he was expected to be a developmental pick and that has held true. Through 9 games, Morris has played one snap on offense and 19 on special teams. Morris has been active the last seven weeks but hasn't needed to play. If this season continues to spiral, maybe we'll get to see what Morris can do.

Round 7-244: Cole Wisniewski, S, Texas Tech

Because Cole Wisniewski missed most of training camp with injuries (hamstring and concussion), he never really had much of a shot to make the Eagles' 53-man roster. But once Wisniewski cleared waivers, the Eagles signed him to the practice squad and he has been there all season. Through nine games, Wisniewski has been elevated twice and has played 36 snaps on special teams. He has one elevation remaining.

The Eagles knew Uar Bernard was a long-term project when they drafted him and that is playing out. Bernard has next-level athleticism but is extremely raw so he needs to continue to learn the game. After the Eagles waived him at final cuts, they signed him to the practice squad. Unfortunately, Bernard suffered an injury in practice and has been on the Practice Squad Injured list back on Oct. 9. So this project is on hold for now.

Round 7-252: Keyshawn James-Newby, Edge, New Mexico

There was a lot of hand-wringing from fans in the summer when the Eagles attempted to get both Bernard and Josh Weru through waivers to the practice squad. The Eagles were probably more concerned about Keyshawn James-Newby. Because even though the seventh-round pick missed most of training camp with an ankle injury, he did flash in his preseason action. But James-Newby cleared waivers and has been on the practice squad all season.

The Eagles have used up two of his three elevations this season. James-Newby played 48 defensive snaps in his NFL debut against the Panthers and then 4 the following week against the Panthers. He has a couple of combined tackles in his defensive action. He has also played 40 special teams snaps.

The Eagles signed the 24-year-old long snapper after the draft and he has played in every game this season — totaling 77 special teams snaps. He has 3 combined tackles and a fumble recovery. He was able to show some hustle when he jumped on a muffed punt against the Giants. Underwood appears to be working well with punter Braden Mann and kicker Jake Elliott.

#2825. Apple, Alphabet and Uber survived major founder transitions. Nvidia will be a harder test

Jensen Huang is not the first CEO to leave a company he helped build.

But experts told CNBC that his sudden resignation from Nvidia at a critical juncture is unique and leaves his eventual successor with challenges in leading the company that's powering the artificial intelligence boom.

In 33 years, Huang, who resigned on Thursday amid reports of extramarital affairs, including with a woman U.S. intelligence believes is a spy for China, made the company he co-founded the world's most valuable.

Former Goldman Sachs CEO Lloyd Blankfein wrote on X that he was "indispensable" but "the graveyards are filled with indispensable men," referencing a quote attributed to French leader Charles de Gaulle.

While the board looks for a new CEO and CFO Colette Kress does the job temporarily; analysts say Huang's charisma and bullish drive will be hard to replicate.

"I'm not sure we've seen a situation quite like this, where the founder and CEO of one of the world's largest companies faces such a sudden departure amid scandal," Michael Withers, professor of management at the University of Notre Dame's Mendoza College of Business, told CNBC.

Whoever follows will "face challenges that go well beyond those of a typical CEO replacing a founder," he added.

Apple's image was inseparable from Steve Jobs when he resigned in 2011. But unlike Nvidia, the company had had other CEOs — and an internal successor.

Tim Cook invested in Apple's chip strategy and services business and became a key corporate diplomat in dealings with the U.S. and Chinese governments. He was known as an operator who controlled Apple's supply chain.

Cook had "considerable preparation" for becoming CEO, Withers said. Apple's market cap went from about $350 billion to $4 trillion in his 15-year tenure.

Larry Page and Sergey Brin left their executive roles at Alphabet in 2019, 21 years after founding Google.

Page and Brin's departures were expected. Page said at the time that it was the "natural time" to simplify the company's management structure. Alphabet shares are up more than 300% since then under Sunder Pichai.

Travis Kalanick left Uber in 2017 after mounting pressure over a series of scandals and governance concerns.

But his departure "unfolded over several months, and while it certainly attracted attention, it pales in comparison to what Nvidia is currently facing," Withers added.

Former Expedia CEO Dara Khosrowshahi repaired the organization and found a path to profitability.

Withers said successor CEOs often struggle "under normal circumstances" to juggle maintaining what made a company successful while establishing their own leadership.

"It becomes considerably more difficult when the transition follows such a high-profile scandal involving the company's founder," he added.

Huang had an extraordinary 60 direct reports, as part of a flat structure that favored fast communication over layered hierarchies.

Withers told CNBC Nvidia did not appear to have a succession plan for Huang, who is also often described as inextricably linked to the company.

"We're talking about a company that was, until recently, the largest in the world by market capitalization. That's a pretty remarkable situation," he added.

As well as having to "step out of his shadow," the new CEO will have to deal with the "scandal" arising from Huang's conduct and "the extent to which it sticks with Nvidia," Withers said.

Huang's successor will also have to tackle the longer-term business strategy, while picking up the relationships he managed.

Huang cultivated a close relationship with U.S. President Donald Trump and accompanied him to China in May. Huang consistently pushed for the U.S. to relax export restrictions that prevent Nvidia from selling its chips to the world's second-largest economy.

He also managed close relationships with the bosses of the company's key suppliers, from SK Hynix to Samsung.

Storm Uru, co-head of the Global Innovation team at Nvidia investor Liontrust Asset Management, said two key priorities were changing company culture and managing the supply chain.

The supply chain was "where Jensen was able to provide a vision for the future and able to bring people to the table and to expand capacity to enable the build-out of this technology," Uru told CNBC, adding it mattered especially "when supply chains are already so tight."

Nvidia continues to face rising competition from rivals such as AMD and hyperscalers, which are ramping up efforts to develop their own chips.

Investors noted that the new CEO will have a roadmap of products for the next few years, unaffected by Huang's removal.

"We have to look beyond Jensen ... there are thousands upon thousands of engineers," Joanne Feeney, partner and portfolio manager, Advisors Capital Management, told CNBC on Thursday, as pressure was mounting on Huang to go.

#2823. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

Moore ranks No. 142 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.4%. See Nvidia Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Aerospace company SpaceX recently announced solid third-quarter results, with its AI revenue surging to $7.56 billion from $813 million in the prior-year quarter.

Impressed by the results, Needham analyst Ryan Koontz reiterated a buy rating on SPCX stock with a price target of $250. Koontz highlighted that SpaceX's Q3 results significantly exceeded expectations, proving that the company's vertically integrated business model is driving massive scale.

Furthermore, Koontz emphasized the 195.2% sequential growth in SpaceX's AI revenue, fueled by wholesale spot compute deals. He noted management's increasing focus on its AI strategy, given that it is optimistic about adding 10 gigawatts of incremental capacity during 2027.

"With increasing confidence on Starship re-use, we meaningfully increase our estimates," said Koontz.

Koontz ranks No. 357 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 42% of the time, delivering an average return of 23.7%. See SpaceX Technical Analysis on TipRanks.
